2. More sophisticated: Minimize smooth approximation L = (max-ish) e.g. objective = (sum_k exp(beta * c_k / sum^2))/exp(beta) or logsumexp. Since scale fixed sum=1, minimize logsumexp(beta*c_k). Let's explore local/mirror descent.

Define a nonnegative normalized sum=1. Convolution c = np.convolve(a,a). Objective: max c (score = 2n max). Need reduce peak. We can use gradient subproblem: choose active large lags K, gradient g_i = sum_{k in K} w_k h_{k,i}, where h_{k,i}=a_{k-i} if 0≤k-i<n. This is correlation of a with shifted versions: for each active lag k, contribution to a_i is a_{k-i}. Then update a_i -= eta g_i. To keep nonnegative, projected. Since c changes quadratically: dc_k/d a_i = a_{k-i}. If we reduce a_i weighted by those that produce peaks, can flatten.

A natural algorithm: Multiplicative subgradient:
For each iteration:
- c = conv(a,a); S = sum c (should 1)
- Identify lags with c above threshold (top q or all > τ*max)
- For selected lags, form gradient g via accumulation: for each lag k with weight w, for i range max(0,k-n+1)..min(n-1,k): g[i] += w*a[k-i]
- Update a_i *= exp(-eta * g_i / (max a?)) or a_i = max(0, a_i - eta*g_i). Normalize sum=1.
This is like sink-cut or power method. Need tune eta.

Alternative: Minimize smooth F_beta = logsumexp(beta*c) (since sum c fixed =1). Gradient: p_k = exp(beta c_k)/Z; g_i = ∑_k p_k a_{k-i}. Update a_i proportionally to 1/(g_i) via mirror descent on simplex: a_i *= exp(-eta g_i), normalize. This is like iterative reweighted. If beta large, focuses on peaks.

Since c_k are convex? c_k is quadratic, not concave. But logsumexp of quadratics. Mirror descent might converge to local minima. Need good initialization and restarts.

Let's define a family generator:
```
def make_inv_sqrt(n, alpha=1.0, beta=0.5, gamma=0.0, lam=0.0):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    base=(i+alpha)**(-beta)
    if lam!=0: base *= np.exp(-lam*i/n)
    if gamma!=0: base *= (i/n+1e-12)**(-gamma)?
    base=np.maximum(base,0)
    if sum<=0: base=ones
    base/=base.sum()
    return base
```
But gamma parameter if positive right singular may cause center large for n large if gamma>0? Avoid or small.

Main loop:
```
no_improve=0
for iter in range(max_iter):
   c = convolve_full(a)
   max_c = c.max()
   score=2*n*max_c
   # active selection:
   frac = 0.9 or dynamic
   high = np.where(c >= max_c - tol)[0]
   if len(high)>max_high: sample max_high evenly/high random
   else use high
   weights = exp((c[high]-max_c)/scale) maybe scale=max_c*0.1
   # compute g slices
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for k in high:
      w=weights[...]
      start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
      jstart=k-end; jstop=k-start
      g[start:end+1] += w * a[jstart:jstop+1][::-1]
   g_mean = g.mean()
   if g_mean==0: break
   g_norm = (g - g_mean?) or g/g_mean
   # line search over steps
   candidates=[]
   for theta in steps: # [0.01,0.03,0.06,0.1,0.2,0.4]
       if method mult:
          new = a * np.exp(-theta * (g_norm - 1.0)) ???
       else additive:
          new = a - theta * (g_norm - 1.0)
          new = np.clip(new,0,1000)
       s=new.sum(); if s<=0: continue
       new/=s
       # maybe add small eps to zero to keep rank? new += 1e-15? normalize
       new_score=score_obj(new)
       choose best
   if improved: a=new; no_improve=0 else no_improve++
   if no_improve>30: apply random perturbation to best and reset; or try different active set/step.
```

`def conv_full(a):`
- Ensure a 1D np float64 contiguous.
- Return np.convolve(a, a, mode='full')? Use `mode='full'`. For speed and exactness, direct. But for many line search, direct may be slower. We can implement `score_fft(a)` using FFT:
```
def conv_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    L=1
    while L < 2*n-1: L <<= 1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    return c
```
For nonnegative, small negative from roundoff: `c = np.clip(c,0,None)` maybe. Scipy fftconvolve could be faster but not guaranteed? Numpy rfft fine. L for n=8192: 2n-1=16383, L=16384. FFT length 8192 rfft? Actually rfft length 16384 => 8193 freq. Very fast. For gradient correlation p length 2n-1 and a_rev length n, full length 3n-2=24574, L=32768. rfft length 32768.

We can use FFT for search score, and direct final verify. But line search picking based on FFT might be slightly inconsistent. Rounding errors ~1e-16, not affect relative 1e-4. Good.

Potential performance: Calling np.fft.rfft on 8192/16384 many times. 1000 times okay. We can cache FFT length? a changes so no.

`def objective(a):` uses fft? Let's create:
```
def score_direct(a):
    c=np.convolve(a,a,'full')
    s=a.sum()
    return 2.0*a.size*float(c.max())/(s*s)

def score_fft(a):
    c=conv_fft(a)
    s=a.sum()
    return 2.0*a.size*float(np.max(c))/(s*s)
```
Use score_fft for inner loops; score_direct periodically and final candidates. Since a nonnegative small, sum~1.

Gradient via FFT:
```
def gradient_from_weights(p,a):
    # p length 2n-1
    a_rev = a[::-1]
    length = len(p) + len(a_rev) - 1  # 3n-2
    L=1
    while L < length: L <<=1
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    Fa=np.fft.rfft(a_rev, L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fa, L)[length]  # full conv length = length; slice n-1:2n-1
    g = out[n-1: 2*n-1]
    # Due round tiny negative? p,a nonnegative but convolution positive; clip.
    if g.min()<0: g[g<0]=0
    return g
```

Line search details with smooth method:
```
def mirror_step(a, g_norm, theta):
    # clip exponent to avoid overflow
    factor = -theta * (g_norm - 1.0)
    factor = np.clip(factor, -10.0, 10.0)
    new = a * np.exp(factor)
    s=new.sum()
    if s <=0: return None
    new /= s
    return new
```
Since g_norm mean 1 but not exactly? Use actual mean. If gm positive, g_norm=g/gm.

- `def _make_random_smooth(n, rng, base=None, sigma):` generate low-frequency log noise. Use random Fourier:
```
def _smooth_noise(n, rng, n_freqs=32):
    freqs = np.fft.rfftfreq(n)*n  # cycles
    # low freq power
    amp = rng.normal(0,1,size=len(freqs)) * (1/(1+freqs+1e-3))
    noise = np.fft.irfft(amp, n)
    return noise
```
No lambda. Candidate = base * exp(noise*sigma) normalized.

def smooth_iteration(a, temp_factor, theta, n):
    c = _conv_fft(a)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <=0: return None, None, score?
    temp = max(cmax * temp_factor, 1e-300)
    p = np.exp((c - cmax)/temp)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: p = np.full(len(c),1/len(c)); 
    p /= s
    g = _gradient_p(p,a)
    gm = float(g.mean())
    if gm <=0: return None,None
    g_norm = g/gm
    # Optionally center g_norm: g_norm = g_norm - 1? Multiplicative with centered already:
    factor = -theta * (g_norm - 1.0)
    factor = np.clip(factor, -8.0, 8.0)
    new = a * np.exp(factor)
    new = _normalize(new)
    return new
```

Normalization: `_normalize(a)`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
        return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0) # values ~1e-3
    a /= s
    return a
```
Clipping after divide? If s small? s=1. Values small.

Need handle if `np.exp` overflow: factor clip.

Score functions:
```
def _score_fft(a):
    c = _conv_fft(a)
    if c.size == 0: return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf # search normalized 1
    return float(2.0 * a.size * cmax / (s*s))
```
Since we normalize to 1, denominator 1; but keep.

`_conv_fft(a)`:
```
def _conv_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    need=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < need: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:need]
    idx = c < 0
    if np.any(idx):
        c[idx] = 0.0
    return c
```
Potential slight imaginary? no.

Could disable factor for c because clipping small negatives maybe for a with true zero? nonnegative; small neg error. Good.

Gradient function:
```
def _gradient_with_p(p,a):
    n=a.size
    ar=a[::-1]
    need=len(p)+n-1
    L=1
    while L < need: L <<=1
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(ar, L)
    full=np.fft.irfft(fp*fa, L)[:need]
    g=full[n-1:2*n-1].copy()
    g[g<0]=0.0
    return g
```
Check if p length maybe all zeros? no.

Potential improvement: We can compute p and g in real space for small n? fine.

Smooth step with line search:
```
def _smooth_line_search(a, score, temp_factor, thetas):
    n=a.size
    c=_conv_fft(a)
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    if cmax <=0: return a,score,1e-3
    temp=max(cmax*temp_factor, cmax*1e-12, 1e-300)
    p=np.exp((c-cmax)/temp)
    ps=float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <= 0:
       p=np.zeros_like(c); p[np.argmax(c)]=1.0
    else: p /= ps
    g=_gradient_with_p(p,a)
    gm=float(np.sum(g))/n
    if gm <=0 or not finite:
       return a,score,1e-3
    g_norm=g/gm
    # optionally stabilize: g_norm = 1 + 0.5*(g_norm-1) if variance huge? no.
    best_a=a; best_score=score; best_theta=thetas[0]
    for th in thetas:
       factor = -th * (g_norm - 1.0)
       factor = np.clip(factor, -10.0, 10.0)
       new = a * np.exp(factor)
       new = _normalize(new)
       sc = _score_fft(new)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
           best_score=sc; best_a=new; best_theta=th
    return best_a,best_score,best_theta
```
We recompute score for candidate; if no improvement, return old. The line search may choose a candidate with FFT score slightly lower due rounding; to avoid infinite chasing, require improvement > 1e-12 relative. In caller, update if sc < best_score - 1e-12. But line search returns best anyway. If best_score improvement tiny, no update.

But line search cycles can be improved by using golden section? We'll keep list.

Thetas dynamic: We can define in caller:
```
step = 0.1

Let's consider random perturbation function with low frequency. Need avoid complex inverse with wrong normalization. Numpy irfft amplifies; noise amplitude okay. We only need shape.
```
def _random_log_noise(n, rng, order=16):
    m = n//2+1
    coeffs = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=m)
    # emphasize low freq
    weights = 1.0 / np.maximum(1.0, np.fft.rfftfreq(n)*n)
    coeffs *= weights
    noise = np.fft.irfft(coeffs, n)
    noise /= np.linalg.norm(noise, ord=2) + 1e-12
    return noise
```
Maybe too much code. Simpler: block multiplicative perturb like previous but smooth. Use random block scaling on log:
```
def _block_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    new=a.copy()
    num_blocks = 16
    block = max(1, n//num_blocks)
    for _ in range(num_blocks):
        start=rng.integers(0,n-block+1)
        length=rng.integers(max(1,block//4), block+1)
        new[start:start+length] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
    return _normalize(new)
```
This is easy and top-level. Use for perturbations.

def _generate_pool(n, rng):
   pool=[]
   # central root
   add(_central_root(n))
   # powers
   for alpha in [0.5,1.0,2.0]: add(_make_power(n,0.5,alpha))
   for beta in [0.45,0.49,0.52]: add(_make_power(n,beta,1.0))
   for lam in [0.5,1.0,2.0]: add(_make_power(n,0.5,1.0,lam))
   for tilt in [-0.5,0.5]: add(_make_power(n,0.5,1.0, shape_power=tilt))
   # block perturb
   base=_central_root(n)
   for sig in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]:
       for rep in range(2): add(_block_perturb(base,rng,sigma=sig))
   return pool
```

`_make_power`: Parameters `lambda_decay` and `tilt` maybe
```
def _make_power(n, beta=0.5, alpha=1.0, decay=0.0, tilt=0.0):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
    x=i/n
    val=(i+alpha)**(-beta)
    if decay !=0: val *= np.exp(-decay*x)
    if tilt !=0: val *= (1.0 + tilt*x)  # ensure positive: if 1+tilt*x can be negative for tilt<-1; limit tilt>-1.
    val=np.maximum(val,1e-300)
    return _normalize(val)
```
Tilt negative down to -0.5 okay.

Refine implementation details:
```
def _refine_sequence(a, score, deadline, rng, tag=''):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score = float(score)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score
    work = a.copy(); work_score = score
    step = 0.1
    iter_count = 0
    no_improve = 0
    temp_widen = 1.0
    print(f"{tag} start score {score:.9f}")
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        # choose temp_factor
        base_temp = 0.3 * math.exp(-iter_count / 150.0)
        if base_temp < 0.003: base_temp = 0.003
        temp_factor = base_temp * temp_widen
        if temp_factor > 0.8: temp_factor=0.8
        if temp_factor < 0.001: temp_factor=0.001
        # thetas
        thetas = []
        for mult in [0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0]:
            t=step*mult
            if t in ...? no set lambda; manual append if not close.
        # Add fixed small/large
        for t in [0.002,0.01,0.2,0.8]: append if unique
        sort thetas? Need sort without lambda: `thetas.sort()` is fine no lambda.
        # Maybe limit to 8
        new, new_score, new_step = _smooth_line_search(work, work_score, temp_factor, thetas[:8])
        if new_score < work_score - 1e-12:
            work = new; work_score = new_score
            if work_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = work_score; best_a = work.copy()
                print(tag + " improve ...")
            no_improve = 0
            step = new_step if new_step>1e-4 else step
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            # Revert work to best if perturbation not improving
            if no_improve > 3:
                work = best_a.copy()
                work_score = best_score
            if no_improve > 8:
                # random perturbation from best
                sigma = 0.15 * math.exp(-no_improve/25.0)
                if sigma < 0.01: sigma = 0.01
                new = _block_perturb(best_a, rng, sigma)
                sc = _score_fft(new)
                if finite and sc < best_score: update...
                work = new; work_score=sc
                no_improve=0
                # widen temp
                temp_widen = min(10.0, temp_widen*1.5)
            else:
                temp_widen = min(10.0, temp_widen*1.1)
        iter_count += 1
        # periodic direct check
        if iter_count % 100 == 0:
            direct = _score_direct(best_a)
            if finite:
                if direct < best_score:
                    best_score = direct
                # If FFT was optimistic and direct worse, set best_score=direct? Actually if direct > best_score, set to direct for true.
                best_score = direct
                # If direct much worse, maybe best_a still same; update work? no.
        # If no improve too many and time, maybe break to restart? But phase recursion handles.
    return best_a, best_score
```

def _append_unique(list, value, existing):
    if value <=0: return list
    for x in existing:
       if abs(value-x) <= 1e-12*max(1,abs(x)): return list

```
def _make_tapered(n, beta=0.5, alpha=1.0, poly_tilt=0.0, exp_tilt=0.0):
    i=np.arange(n,float); x=i/n
    val=(i+alpha)**(-beta)
    if poly_tilt !=0:
       base=1.0 + poly_tilt*x
       if np.any(base <=0): base=np.maximum(base,1e-6)
       val *= base
    if exp_tilt !=0:
       # exp_tilt can be positive to increase to right, negative for decay
       val *= np.exp(np.clip(exp_tilt*x, -20,20))
    return _normalize(val)
```

LP dual covering: minimize M∑ y_k s.t. (y*a)_j ≥1. To maximize sum d, y put on lags that restrict. Mixing with d maybe like gradient descent. Could be effective.

Could implement a custom projected gradient on LP dual? The LP itself is convex. We can solve primal via multiplicative weights (MW) for packing:
Var d_j, constraints c_k(d)=∑_i a_i d_{k-i} ≤ M. Max ∑d. MW for packing: initialize d=1. Iterate: constraints c_k/M; weights w_k=exp(η c_k/M); update d_j *= exp(-η ∑_k w_k ∂c_k/∂d_j/M?) The gradient of constraints wrt d_j is a_{k-j}. So update d_j *= exp(-η ∑_k w_k a_{k-j}/M). This is similar to our mirror descent but with constraints only and w from active constraints, and objective maximize sum d. The update direction is multiplicative on variables with gradient of weighted constraints. This is exactly subgradient of max constraints but uses exponential weights on constraints, perhaps better. It doesn't normalize d each time? We can iterate. After getting d, mix with a.

Our smooth all-lag mirror descent on max(a*a) uses weights p_k=softmax(β c_k) and updates a_i *= exp(-η p_k∂c_k/∂a_i). The LP packing update uses d variables not a, and constraints from fixed a, not c(a). That is a different step: compute d from a, then a_new = mix(a,d). Could be more stable and maybe more powerful.

Let's implement an LP-inspired iteration:
Given a (sum=1), M=max(a*a). Use multiplicative weights to solve primal packing approximately for a fixed number T (e.g., 50) iterations:
- d = ones(n) (or a copy)
- constraints c = convolution(a,d) length 2n-1; scale by M.
- weights w = exp(η * c/M) maybe normalized? For packing, update variables d_j *= exp(-η * grad_j / M?) where grad_j = ∑_k w_k ∂c_k/∂d_j = ∑_k w_k a_{k-j}. This is analogous but with w on constraints c/M. After updates, normalize? We need d nonnegative, no normalization in LP; can rescale after. Use η e.g., 1.
- Repeat T times computing c via FFT? c=conv(a,d) (different lengths) and gradient. We already have gradient function for weights p length 2n-1 and variable d; same `_gradient_with_p(p,d)` where p = softmax of c? Actually p_w = exp(η c_k/M). Then grad = correlation(p_w,d). Update d *= exp(-η grad /?); normalize? To keep numbers, maybe after each update divide by mean or max.
At end, normalize d to sum1. Mix: a_new = (1-t)a + t d. Use line search t.

Implement `_safe_array` for height_sequence_1:
```
def _array_from_global(name):
    obj = globals().get(name, None)
    arr = None
    if obj is not None:
       try:
          arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64)
       except Exception:
          arr = None
    if arr is not None:
       arr = np.ravel(arr)
       arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
       s=arr.sum()
       if arr.size>0 and s>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
          arr = arr/s
    return arr
```

```
def _add_candidate(pool, a, min_n=64, max_n=20000):
    if a is None: return
    try: a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except: return
    if a.size < min_n or a.size > max_n: return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: return
    a=a/s
    pool.append(a)
```

Let's test alignment with a small example manually:
n=2, a=[a0,a1], p=[p0,p1,p2]. a_rev=[a1,a0]. conv full length 4:
index0: p0*a1
1: p0*a0 + p1*a1
2: p1*a0 + p2*a1
3: p2*a0
We want g0 = p0*a0 + p1*a1? Because g_i=∑_j p_{i+j} a_j. For i=0: j=0 p0 a0; j=1 p1 a1. That is conv index1: p0*a0 + p1*a1. Slice n-1=1 to 3: indices [1,2]. index2 = p1*a0 + p2*a1. For i=1: j=0 p1 a1? Wait formula g_i=∑_k p_k a_{k-i}; for i=1: k from1..2? k=1 ->p1 a0, k=2 ->p2 a1. That is p1 a0 + p2 a1. Yes index2. But j mapping in g_i=∑_j p[i+j] a_j: for i=1,j=0 p1 a0, j=1 p2 a1. Good. Our slice [n-1:2n-1] = [1:3] gives g. Good.

For p all ones, n=3: g computed correctly.

One subtle: p length 2n-1, a_rev length n; full convolution length 3n-2. Slice [n-1:2n-1] length n. Good.

Let's draft code in mind:
```
"""Search using smooth log-sum-exp mirror descent from one-sided singular priors..."""
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def _pow2_len(need):
    if need <=1: return 1
    return 1 << int(need - 1).bit_length()

def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size ==0: return np.ones(1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    a = a / s
    return a
```
Using np.where no lambda.

`_conv_fft`:
```
def _conv_fft(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    need=2*n-1
    L=_pow2_len(need)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:need]
    if c.size:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
`np.maximum(c,0.0)` returns new; fine.

`_score_fft`:
```
def _score_fft(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    c=_conv_fft(a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01: return np.inf # search sum1
    if not np.isfinite(cmax): return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s*s))
```
For search, s~1; sum check okay. If a sum normalized 1.

`_score_direct` similar but `np.convolve(a,a,'full')`.

`_gradient_with_p`:
```
def _gradient_with_p(p,a):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0 or p.size==0: return np.zeros(n)
    ar=a[::-1]
    need=p.size + n -1
    L=_pow2_len(need)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(ar, L)
    full=np.fft.irfft(fp*fa, L)[:need]
    g=full[n-1:2*n-1]
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return g
```

`_make_power`:
```
def _make_power(n, beta=0.5, alpha=1.0, poly_tilt=0.0, exp_tilt=0.0):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x=i/float(n)
    base=i+float(alpha)
    if beta != 1.0:
        val=np.power(np.maximum(base, 1e-6), -float(beta))
    else:
        val=np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if poly_tilt !=0.0:
        factor=1.0 + float(poly_tilt)*x
        factor=np.maximum(factor, 1e-6)
        val *= factor
    if exp_tilt != 0.0:
        val *= np.exp(np.clip(float(exp_tilt)*x, -20.0, 20.0))
    return _normalize(val)
```
If poly_tilt negative large, factor min. Use tilts > -1.

`_make_central_root`:
```
def _make_central_root(n):
    a=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    a[0]=1.0
    for k in range(1,n):
       a[k]=a[k-1] * (2.0*k -1.0) / (2.0*k)
    return _normalize(a)
```
For n large loop Python 8192, fine.

`_root_with_tilt`:
```
def _root_with_tilt(n, poly_tilt=0.0, exp_tilt=0.0):
    a=_make_central_root(n)
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)/n
    if poly_tilt !=0:
       factor=np.maximum(1.0+poly_tilt*x,1e-6)
       a *= factor
    if exp_tilt !=0:
       a *= np.exp(np.clip(exp_tilt*x,-20,20))
    return _normalize(a)
```

`_block_perturb`:
```
def _block_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.1):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return _normalize(a)
    num_blocks = 12 + int(np.sqrt(n//16)? no sqrt import? math.sqrt)
```
Use simple number of blocks 24 for large.
```
    num_blocks = max(4, min(64, n // 128 + 4))
    for _ in range(num_blocks):
       length = int(rng.integers(max(1, n//512), max(2, n//16)))
       if length >= n: length = n
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - length + 1)))
       stop = min(n, start+length)
       factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
       a[stop? start:stop] *= factor
    return _normalize(a)
```
If length <1? use max.

def _build_pool(n, rng):
    pool=[]
    _add_candidate(pool, _make_central_root(n))
    for alpha in (0.5,1.0,2.0): _add_candidate(pool,_make_power(n,0.5,alpha))
    for beta in (0.42,0.46,0.49,0.51,0.54): _add_candidate(pool,_make_power(n,beta,1.0))
    for tilt in (-0.25,-0.1,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
       _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n,0.5,1.0,poly_tilt=tilt))
    for et in (-0.5,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
       _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n,0.5,1.0,exp_tilt=et))
    for tilt in (0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0):
       _add_candidate(pool, _root_with_tilt(n, poly_tilt=tilt))
       _add_candidate(pool, _root_with_tilt(n, exp_tilt=tilt))
    for sig in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4):
       for rep in range(2):
           _add_candidate(pool, _block_perturb(_make_central_root(n), rng, sig))
    return pool
```

`_smooth_line_search`: Need thetas list maybe global constant. Define bottom-level `_SMOOTH_THETAS` tuple okay no lambda.
```
_SMOOTH_THETAS = (0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0)
```
In line search, maybe limit to 8 to save: `thetas=_SMOOTH_THETAS[:8]`. But variable? top-level.

```
def _smooth_line_search(a, score, temp_factor):
   n=a.size
   c=_conv_fft(a)
   cmax=float(np.max(c))
   if cmax <=0: return a, score, 0.01
   temp=max(cmax*temp_factor, cmax*1e-12, 1e-300)
   p=np.exp((c-cmax)/temp)
   ps=float(np.sum(p))
   if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <=0:
      p=np.zeros_like(c); p[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
   else: p /= ps
   g=_gradient_with_p(p,a)
   gm=float(np.mean(g))
   if not np.isfinite(gm) or gm <=0:
      return a,score,0.01
   g_norm=g/gm
   best_a=a; best_score=score; best_theta=0.01
   for theta in _SMOOTH_THETAS:
      factor = -theta*(g_norm - 1.0)
      factor=np.clip(factor, -12.0, 12.0)
      new = a * np.exp(factor)
      new=_normalize(new)
      sc=_score_fft(new)
      if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
          best_a=new; best_score=sc; best_theta=theta
   return best_a,best_score,best_theta
```
It includes 9 scores. Could include 2.0? Maybe not.

In `_refine_sequence`, if no improve for many, we might want larger steps; the fixed includes 1.0. Good.

`_refine_sequence` code:
```
def _refine_sequence(a, score, deadline, rng, tag=''):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score=float(score)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
       score=_score_fft(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
    work=a.copy(); work_score=score
    step? not used
    no_improve=0
    iter_count=0
    widen=1.0
    last_direct=0
    print(tag + " refine start " + format(score,'.9f'))
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now=time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline: break
        # temp factor
        base_temp=0.35 * math.exp(-float(iter_count)/120.0)
        if base_temp < 0.004: base_temp=0.004
        temp_factor=base_temp*widen
        if temp_factor >0.8: temp_factor=0.8
        elif temp_factor <0.001: temp_factor=0.001
        new, new_score, theta = _smooth_line_search(work, work_score, temp_factor)
        if new_score < work_score - 1e-12:
            work=new; work_score=new_score
            if work_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_a=work.copy(); best_score=work_score
                no_improve=0
                print(tag + " improve iter=%d score=%.9f theta=%.4f"%(iter_count,best_score,theta))
            else:
                no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 3:
                work=best_a.copy(); work_score=best_score
            if no_improve > 10:
                sigma = 0.12 * math.exp(-float(no_improve)/20.0)
                if sigma < 0.01: sigma=0.01
                pert = _block_perturb(best_a, rng, sigma)
                scp = _score_fft(pert)
                if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_a=pert.copy(); best_score=scp
                else:
                    # take perturbation as work even if worse; may help escape
                    pass
                work=pert.copy(); work_score=scp if np.isfinite(scp) else best_score
                no_improve=0
                widen = min(8.0, widen*1.5)
            else:
                widen = min(8.0, widen*1.15)
        iter_count +=1
        # direct verification every 80 or when best improved? Could be expensive. Use 100.
        if iter_count % 80 == 0:
            direct = _score_direct(best_a)
            if np.isfinite(direct):
                if direct < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score=direct
                elif direct > best_score + 1e-9:
                    # FFT was optimistic; reset true score
                    best_score=direct
                # after direct reset, set work to best to avoid chasing non-improvement
                work=best_a.copy(); work_score=best_score
            print(tag + " iter=%d fft=%.9f direct=%.9f"%(iter_count, work_score, direct))
    return best_a,best_score
```
If direct is worse than FFT, we set best_score direct. But a candidate that FFT thought better but direct worse may be lost; okay. However if direct check sets best_score higher, future may find lower. But `work=best_a` with true score. Good.

`_propose`:

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(0.0,float(budget_s))
    deadline=start + max(0.0, budget - 10.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # determine n trials
    if budget > 1200:
       n_trials=[2048,4096,8192]
    elif budget > 600:
       n_trials=[1024,2048,4096]
    else:
       n_trials=[512,1024,2048]
    pool=[]
    for n in n_trials:
       pool.extend(_build_pool(n,rng))
    # add global
    h=_array_from_global('height_sequence_1')
    if h is not None and 256 <= h.size <= 20000:
       _add_candidate(pool,h)
    # also constant fallback in pool maybe for each n? not needed.
    if not pool:
       return [1.0]
    scored=[]
    for cand in pool:
       sc=_score_fft(cand)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
          scored.append((sc,cand))
    if not scored:
       return [1.0]
    scored.sort(key=_sort_score_item)
    # direct verify top 8
    top_k=min(8,len(scored))
    for idx in range(top_k):
       sc,cand=scored[idx]
       dsc=_score_direct(cand)
       if np.isfinite(dsc): sc=dsc
       scored[idx]=(sc,cand)
    scored.sort(key=_sort_score_item)
    best_a=scored[0][1].copy(); best_score=scored[0][0]
    print("initial best n=%d score=%.9f"%(best_a.size,best_score))
    # phases
    phase_specs=[]
    if len(scored)>0: phase_specs.append((0,0.55))
    if len(scored)>1: phase_specs.append((1,0.18))
    if len(scored)>2: phase_specs.append((2,0.14))
    if len(scored)>3: phase_specs.append((3,0.10))
    # if only few, adjust fractions? The sum may <1; use remaining for best? We can refine top1 longer if len<2.
    # We'll compute deadlines relative to initial time.
    initial_now=time.monotonic()
    remaining=max(0.0, deadline - initial_now - 5.0)
    used=0.0
    for idx, frac in phase_specs:
        if idx >= len(scored): continue
        phase_deadline = start + (budget - 10.0) * frac? Better: now + remaining*frac?
```
Simpler: Use `end_of_phase` accumulating:
```
    phase_end = initial_now
    total_time = max(0.0, deadline - initial_now - 5.0)
    for idx, frac in phase_specs:

       phase_end += frac

       best_phase_a, best_phase_score = _refine_sequence(initial_a, initial_score, phase_end, rng, tag=f'phase{idx}')
       if best_phase_score < best_score:
          best_a=best_phase_a; best_score=best_phase_score
       initial_now = time.monotonic() # for next? But phase_end precomputed
```

```python
"""
Search algorithm for non-negative step-function heights.

The objective is scale-invariant:
    score(a) = 2 * n * max(a * a) / (sum(a))**2
where the convolution is full linear convolution.  We normalize every
candidate to sum 1 and minimize 2*n*max(conv(a,a)).

Initial priors are based on one-sided singular profiles, especially
a_i ~ (i+alpha)^(-1/2) and the finite square-root of a flat prefix
(Central-binomial coefficients).  These profiles are already lower than
the uniform sequence (score 2).  The main optimizer is a smooth mirror
descent / projected multiplicative update on the simplex:

    p_k = softmax(beta * conv_k)
    g_i = sum_k p_k * d(conv_k)/d a_i
    a_i <- a_i * exp(-theta * (g_i / mean(g) - 1))

with a line search over theta.  Periodic direct evaluations and random
block perturbations are used to fight floating-point optimism and local
stalls.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _pow2_len(need):
    """Smallest power of two >= need."""
    if need <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << int(need - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(a):
    """Return a non-negative normalized copy of a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _conv_fft(a):
    """Full convolution a*a using real FFT.  Input is nonnegative."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = _pow2_len(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:need]
    if c.size:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_fft(a):
    """Approximate objective using FFT convolution, sum(a) can be arbitrary."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_fft(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def _score_direct(a):
    """Exact objective using np.convolve, matching the evaluator."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def _gradient_with_p(p, a):
    """
    Given weights p over convolution lags, compute
        g_i = sum_k p_k * a_{k-i}
    for i=0..n-1 where valid.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0 or p.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    ar = a[::-1]
    need = p.size + n - 1
    L = _pow2_len(need)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(ar, L)
    full = np.fft.irfft(fp * fa, L)[:need]
    g = full[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def _make_power(n, beta=0.5, alpha=1.0, poly_tilt=0.0, exp_tilt=0.0):
    """
    Candidate profile:
        (i+alpha)^(-beta) * (1+poly_tilt*i/n) * exp(exp_tilt*i/n)
    normalized.
    """
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = i / float(n)

    if beta == 1.0:
        val = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        base = np.maximum(i + float(alpha), 1e-6)
        val = np.power(base, -float(beta))

    if poly_tilt != 0.0:
        factor = 1.0 + float(poly_tilt) * x
        factor = np.maximum(factor, 1e-6)
        val = val * factor

    if exp_tilt != 0.0:
        val = val * np.exp(np.clip(float(exp_tilt) * x, -20.0, 20.0))

    return _normalize(val)


def _make_central_root(n):
    """
    Coefficients of the finite polynomial square root of
    1 + x + ... + x^(n-1):
        a_0=1, a_k = a_{k-1}*(2k-1)/(2k).
    This makes the first n convolution coefficients exactly flat before
    normalization.
    """
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        a[k] = a[k - 1] * (2.0 * float(k) - 1.0) / (2.0 * float(k))
    return _normalize(a)


def _root_from_base(root, poly_tilt=0.0, exp_tilt=0.0):
    """Apply a smooth one-sided/right-side taper to a base profile."""
    a = np.asarray(root, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return _normalize(a)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    if poly_tilt != 0.0:
        a = a * np.maximum(1.0 + float(poly_tilt) * x, 1e-6)
    if exp_tilt != 0.0:
        a = a * np.exp(np.clip(float(exp_tilt) * x, -20.0, 20.0))
    return _normalize(a)


def _block_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.1):
    """Random smooth-ish multiplicative block perturbation."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return _normalize(a)

    num_blocks = max(6, min(64, int(n / 128) + 6))
    for _ in range(num_blocks):
        low = max(1, n // 512)
        high = max(low + 1, n // 16)
        length = int(rng.integers(low, high))
        if length > n:
            length = n
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - length + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + length)
        if stop > start:
            a[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    return _normalize(a)


def _add_candidate(pool, a, min_n=64, max_n=20000):
    """Validate and append a candidate to the pool."""
    if a is None:
        return
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return
    if arr.size < min_n or arr.size > max_n:
        return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return
    arr = arr / s
    pool.append(arr)


def _build_pool(n, rng):
    """Create a set of promising initial candidates for a fixed length."""
    pool = []

    root = _make_central_root(n)
    _add_candidate(pool, root)

    # One-sided power profiles.
    for alpha in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n, 0.5, alpha))

    # Slight exponent changes.
    for beta in (0.42, 0.46, 0.49, 0.51, 0.54):
        _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n, beta, 1.0))

    # Polynomial right-side tilts.
    for tilt in (-0.25, -0.1, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
        _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n, 0.5, 1.0, poly_tilt=tilt))

    # Exponential right-side tilts; negative decays to the left, positive grows to the right.
    for et in (-0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
        _add_candidate(pool, _make_power(n, 0.5, 1.0, exp_tilt=et))

    # Taper the flat-prefix root profile.
    for tilt in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        _add_candidate(pool, _root_from_base(root, poly_tilt=tilt))
        _add_candidate(pool, _root_from_base(root, exp_tilt=tilt))

    # Random block perturbations of the strong root prior.
    for sig in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4):
        for _ in range(2):
            _add_candidate(pool, _block_perturb(root, rng, sig))

    return pool


def _array_from_global(name):
    """Safely read a global sequence, if present."""
    obj = globals().get(name, None)
    if obj is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr / s


def _score_item(item):
    """Sort key for (score, array) pairs."""
    return item[0]


_SMOOTH_THETAS = (0.002, 0.005, 0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0)


def _smooth_line_search(a, score, temp_factor):
    """
    One mirror-descent / naive-validated step:
    build softmax weights over convolution peaks, compute gradient,
    and line search multiplicative updates.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, score, 0.01

    c = _conv_fft(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return a, score, 0.01

    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return a, score, 0.01

    temp = max(float(cmax) * float(temp_factor), float(cmax) * 1e-12, 1e-300)
    p = np.exp((c - cmax) / temp)
    ps = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros_like(c)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        p = p / ps

    g = _gradient_with_p(p, a)
    gm = float(np.mean(g))
    if not np.isfinite(gm) or gm <= 0.0:
        return a, score, 0.01

    g_norm = g / gm

    best_a = a
    best_score = float(score)
    best_theta = 0.01

    for theta in _SMOOTH_THETAS:
        factor = -theta * (g_norm - 1.0)
        factor = np.clip(factor, -12.0, 12.0)
        new = a * np.exp(factor)
        new = _normalize(new)
        sc = _score_fft(new)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_a = new
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_theta = float(theta)

    return best_a, best_score, best_theta


def _refine_sequence(a, score, deadline, rng, tag=""):
    """Run the main smooth local refinement until deadline."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = _score_fft(a)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = float(score)
    work = a.copy()
    work_score = float(best_score)

    no_improve = 0
    stale_best = 0
    widen = 1.0
    iter_count = 0

    if tag:
        print(f"{tag} refine start score={best_score:.9f}")

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.75:
            break

        base_temp = 0.35 * math.exp(-float(iter_count) / 120.0)
        if base_temp < 0.004:
            base_temp = 0.004
        temp_factor = base_temp * widen
        if temp_factor > 0.8:
            temp_factor = 0.8
        elif temp_factor < 0.001:
            temp_factor = 0.001

        new, new_score, theta = _smooth_line_search(work, work_score, temp_factor)

        if np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < work_score - 1e-12:
            work = new
            work_score = float(new_score)
            if work_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_a = work.copy()
                best_score = work_score
                no_improve = 0
                stale_best = 0
                if tag:
                    print(f"{tag} improve iter={iter_count} score={best_score:.9f} theta={theta:.4f}")
            else:
                stale_best += 1
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            stale_best += 1

        if no_improve > 3:
            work = best_a.copy()
            work_score = float(best_score)

        if stale_best > 8 or no_improve > 10:
            sigma = 0.12 * math.exp(-float(max(0, no_improve)) / 20.0)
            if sigma < 0.01:
                sigma = 0.01
            pert = _block_perturb(best_a, rng, sigma)
            scp = _score_fft(pert)
            if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_a = pert.copy()
                best_score = float(scp)
                if tag:
                    print(f"{tag} perturb improve iter={iter_count} score={best_score:.9f}")

            if np.isfinite(scp):
                work = pert.copy()
                work_score = float(scp)
            else:
                work = best_a.copy()
                work_score = float(best_score)

            no_improve = 0
            stale_best = 0
            widen = min(8.0, widen * 1.5)
        else:
            widen = min(8.0, widen * 1.05)

        iter_count += 1

        # Periodically replace FFT-optimistic scores with exact np.convolve scores.
        if iter_count % 80 == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
            dsc = _score_direct(best_a)
            if np.isfinite(dsc):
                old = best_score
                best_score = float(dsc)
                work = best_a.copy()
                work_score = float(best_score)
                if tag and (abs(best_score - old) > 1e-9 or iter_count % 400 == 0):
                    print(f"{tag} exact iter={iter_count} score={best_score:.9f}")

    return best_a, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-score non-negative sequence within the allotted budget.
    Returns a list of Python floats.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0

    # Leave a safety margin for final validation / serialization.
    deadline = start + max(0.0, budget - 20.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget > 1200.0:
        n_trials = (2048, 4096, 8192)
    elif budget > 600.0:
        n_trials = (1024, 2048, 4096)
    else:
        n_trials = (512, 1024, 2048)

    pool = []
    for n in n_trials:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        try:
            pool.extend(_build_pool(n, rng))
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Include known prior construction if available and reasonably sized.
    prior = _array_from_global("height_sequence_1")
    if prior is not None:
        _add_candidate(pool, prior, min_n=64, max_n=20000)

    if not pool:
        # Safe valid sequence; score is exactly 2.
        return [1.0]

    scored = []
    for cand in pool:
        try:
            sc = _score_fft(cand)
        except Exception:
            sc = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((float(sc), cand))

    if not scored:
        return [1.0]

    scored.sort(key=_score_item)

    # Directly validate the best few primary candidates.
    top_k = min(8, len(scored))
    for idx in range(top_k):
        sc, cand = scored[idx]
        try:
            dsc = _score_direct(cand)
        except Exception:
            dsc = sc
        if np.isfinite(dsc):
            scored[idx] = (float(dsc), cand)
    scored.sort(key=_score_item)

    best_a = np.asarray(scored[0][1], dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = float(scored[0][0])
    print(f"initial best n={best_a.size} score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Refine several diverse promising starting points.
    m = min(4, len(scored))
    if m == 1:
        fracs = (1.0,)
    elif m == 2:
        fracs = (0.65, 0.35)
    elif m == 3:
        fracs = (0.50, 0.27, 0.23)
    else:
        fracs = (0.50, 0.20, 0.17, 0.13)

    total_time = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
    for i in range(m):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        frac = float(fracs[i])
        phase_deadline = time.monotonic() + total_time * frac
        if phase_deadline > deadline:
            phase_deadline = deadline
        if phase_deadline - time.monotonic() < 10.0:
            continue

        init_a = np.asarray(scored[i][1], dtype=np.float64).copy()
        init_s = float(scored[i][0])
        try:
            cand_a, cand_s = _refine_sequence(init_a, init_s, phase_deadline, rng, tag=f"p{i}")
        except Exception:
            cand_a, cand_s = init_a, init_s

        if np.isfinite(cand_s) and cand_s < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_a = np.asarray(cand_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            best_score = float(cand_s)
            print(f"phase {i} improves global score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Final validation against the real evaluator.
    try:
        final_seq = []
        for x in best_a:
            final_seq.append(float(x))
    except Exception:
        final_seq = [1.0]

    try:
        final_score = evaluate_sequence(final_seq)
    except Exception:
        final_score = np.inf

    if np.isfinite(final_score):
        return final_seq

    # Defensive fallbacks.
    try:
        fallback = [1.0]
        fs = evaluate_sequence(fallback)
        if np.isfinite(fs):
            return fallback
    except Exception:
        pass

    return [1.0]
```
